Or close to her, the way I should.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been bothering me. I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I suppose… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. I kept thinking it would sort of pass on its own, but it’s just… stayed around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still go along with things. My wife tries to get us doing our old Sunday routines, and I try to act like I’m enjoying it, because I don’t want to disappoint her. But the feeling just doesn’t really show up.\n\nEven when something good happens at work, I know I should feel pleased, and I can say the right things, but inside it’s kind of flat. I don’t feel real happiness the way I used to… or that closeness with her. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I had normal ups and downs like anyone, but nothing that stuck around like this. This really seems to have started after the retrofit accident, about… a year and seven months ago. I kept hoping it would just settle down, but it hasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of wanting to hurt myself or making any kind of plan. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound more dramatic than it is. I’ve just felt… not myself, I guess. But no, I haven’t been looking to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big thing out of it, but I guess I am on edge a lot.\n\nAt work, when I’m on the floor, I’m constantly checking things—gauges, exits, where people’s hands are, where the emergency stops are. I tell myself it’s just being careful, being responsible, you know? But it’s… probably more than most people are doing.\n\nEven in meetings, I’ll pick a seat where I can see the door. It sounds a little excessive when I say it out loud, but it makes me feel like I can keep track of things. Is that the kind of worry you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since the accident?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been a significant weight loss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some pleasure or interest in things for at least a few days a week?\n\nPatient: I… I still do things, if that counts. Like my wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, and I’ll go along with it. And if something goes well at work, I know I should feel good about it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t really feel real happiness from it. Or closeness, even when she’s trying. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely shut down, but… the feeling just doesn’t seem to come the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this way without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: I guess… since the accident. The retrofit one.\n\nIt’s been about a year and seven months now, if I’m counting right. I kept thinking it would ease up with time, and maybe I’d just get back to normal, but… not much has really changed. Does that answer what you mean by “without improvement”?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of detachment from your wife are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still go through the motions, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just refusing everything or anything like that.\n\nBut the lack of real happiness, or feeling close to my wife… that’s pretty much there every day. Even when she’s trying to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routine, or something at work goes well, it just doesn’t really land the way it should. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt this way before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches like anyone, but this specific feeling — the distance, the lack of real enjoyment — that’s really been since the retrofit accident.\n\nSo… about a year and seven months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t honestly say I remember being this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms have been consistently worse over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “consistently worse,” exactly. The last couple weeks have felt… pretty much like the same bad pattern, if that makes sense.\n\nMaybe some days stand out more than others, but I haven’t really noticed a clear shift where I’d say, “yes, it’s definitely gotten worse.” Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I haven’t really been feeling like myself. It’s not that anything is terribly wrong on paper, you know? I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I suppose… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. I kept thinking it would sort of pass on its own, but it’s just… stayed around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still go along with things. My wife tries to get us doing our old Sunday routines, and I try to act like I’m enjoying it, because I don’t want to disappoint her. But the feeling just doesn’t really show up.\n\nEven when something good happens at work, I know I should feel pleased, and I can say the right things, but inside it’s kind of flat. I don’t feel real happiness the way I used to… or that closeness with her. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not really. factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and trying to help are broken up and partly just gone.\n- key: S072\n  name: Negative_Beliefs\n  description: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: The last couple weeks have felt… pretty much like the same bad pattern, if that makes sense.\n\nMaybe some days stand out more than others, but I haven’t really noticed a clear shift where I’d say, “yes, it’s definitely gotten worse.” Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has your current period of feeling this way lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years. It’s been a long time, but if I’m being accurate, it started after the retrofit accident, so about a year and seven months ago.\n\nThat’s still a while, obviously, but not past two years. Is that the distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you felt normal and your symptoms improved significantly since the accident?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I haven’t really been feeling like myself. It’s not that anything is terribly wrong on paper, you know? My wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, things we used to enjoy, and even when something goes well at work… I just don’t really feel happy about it. Or close to her, the way I should.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been bothering me. I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I suppose… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. I kept thinking it would sort of pass on its own, but it’s just… stayed around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still go along with things. My wife tries to get us doing our old Sunday routines, and I try to act like I’m enjoying it, because I don’t want to disappoint her. But the feeling just doesn’t really show up.\n\nEven when something good happens at work, I know I should feel pleased, and I can say the right things, but inside it’s kind of flat. I don’t feel real happiness the way I used to… or that closeness with her. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I had normal ups and downs like anyone, but nothing that stuck around like this. This really seems to have started after the retrofit accident, about… a year and seven months ago. I kept hoping it would just settle down, but it hasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of wanting to hurt myself or making any kind of plan. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound more dramatic than it is. I’ve just felt… not myself, I guess. But no, I haven’t been looking to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big thing out of it, but I guess I am on edge a lot.\n\nAt work, when I’m on the floor, I’m constantly checking things—gauges, exits, where people’s hands are, where the emergency stops are. I tell myself it’s just being careful, being responsible, you know? But it’s… probably more than most people are doing.\n\nEven in meetings, I’ll pick a seat where I can see the door. It sounds a little excessive when I say it out loud, but it makes me feel like I can keep track of things. Is that the kind of worry you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since the accident?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been a significant weight loss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some pleasure or interest in things for at least a few days a week?\n\nPatient: I… I still do things, if that counts. Like my wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, and I’ll go along with it. And if something goes well at work, I know I should feel good about it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t really feel real happiness from it. Or closeness, even when she’s trying. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely shut down, but… the feeling just doesn’t seem to come the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this way without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: I guess… since the accident. The retrofit one.\n\nIt’s been about a year and seven months now, if I’m counting right. I kept thinking it would ease up with time, and maybe I’d just get back to normal, but… not much has really changed. Does that answer what you mean by “without improvement”?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of detachment from your wife are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still go through the motions, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just refusing everything or anything like that.\n\nBut the lack of real happiness, or feeling close to my wife… that’s pretty much there every day. Even when she’s trying to get me to enjoy our old Sunday routine, or something at work goes well, it just doesn’t really land the way it should. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt this way before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches like anyone, but this specific feeling — the distance, the lack of real enjoyment — that’s really been since the retrofit accident.\n\nSo… about a year and seven months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t honestly say I remember being this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms have been consistently worse over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “consistently worse,” exactly. The last couple weeks have felt… pretty much like the same bad pattern, if that makes sense.\n\nMaybe some days stand out more than others, but I haven’t really noticed a clear shift where I’d say, “yes, it’s definitely gotten worse.” Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has your current period of feeling this way lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years. It’s been a long time, but if I’m being accurate, it started after the retrofit accident, so about a year and seven months ago.\n\nThat’s still a while, obviously, but not past two years. Is that the distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you felt normal and your symptoms improved significantly since the accident?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean… I’ve had maybe a few better days where I could get through things and seem more normal, but I don’t think I’ve felt like myself for a few weeks straight.\n\nIt’s pretty much been going on since the retrofit accident, about a year and seven months ago. I wish I had a better answer than that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: S075\n  name: Social_Detachment\n  description: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.\n  manifestation: At home I sit through family meals like I'm behind glass, hearing people talk and laugh but feeling like I don't really belong in the room with them.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I suppose… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. I kept thinking it would sort of pass on its own, but it’s just… stayed around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still go along with things. My wife tries to get us doing our old Sunday routines, and I try to act like I’m enjoying it, because I don’t want to disappoint her. But the feeling just doesn’t really show up.\n\nEven when something good happens at work, I know I should feel pleased, and I can say the right things, but inside it’s kind of flat. I don’t feel real happiness the way I used to… or that closeness with her. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I had normal ups and downs like anyone, but nothing that stuck around like this. This really seems to have started after the retrofit accident, about… a year and seven months ago. I kept hoping it would just settle down, but it hasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of wanting to hurt myself or making any kind of plan. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound more dramatic than it is. I’ve just felt… not myself, I guess. But no, I haven’t been looking to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big thing out of it, but I guess I am on edge a lot.\n\nAt work, when I’m on the floor, I’m constantly checking things—gauges, exits, where people’s hands are, where the emergency stops are. I tell myself it’s just being careful, being responsible, you know? But it’s… probably more than most people are doing.\n\nEven in meetings, I’ll pick a seat where I can see the door. It sounds a little excessive when I say it out loud, but it makes me feel like I can keep track of things. Is that the kind of worry you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since the accident?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been a significant weight loss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some pleasure or interest in things for at least a few days a week?\n\nPatient: I… I still do things, if that counts. Like my wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, and I’ll go along with it. And if something goes well at work, I know I should feel good about it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t really feel real happiness from it. Or closeness, even when she’s trying. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely shut down, but… the feeling just doesn’t seem to come the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this way without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: I guess… since the accident. The retrofit one.\n\nIt’s been about a year and seven months now, if I’m counting right. I kept thinking it would ease up with time, and maybe I’d just get back to normal, but… not much has really changed. Does that answer what you mean by “without improvement”?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of detachment from your wife are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t honestly say I remember being this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms have been consistently worse over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “consistently worse,” exactly. lie there replaying the plant in my head, and when I do sleep I either jolt awake from dreams about the line or wake up at 3 or 4 in the morning feeling like I never rested.\n- key: S069\n  name: Intrusive_Symptoms\n  description: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: A normal plant alarm, a retrofit checklist, or even the smell of hot metal can throw me right back to seeing him on the floor and hearing everyone yelling for help.\n- key: S070\n  name: Trauma_Avoidance\n  description: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.\n  manifestation: I avoid that production line whenever I can, send someone else to walk it, and shut down conversations when people start talking about the rollout or the investigation.\n- key: S071\n  name: Trauma_Related_Amnesia\n  description: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).\n  manifestation: I can remember the meeting before the failure and the ambulance leaving afterward, but the actual few minutes where I was giving directions and trying to help are broken up and partly just gone.\n- key: S072\n  name: Negative_Beliefs\n  description: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I can't trust my own judgment anymore, like if I miss one detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody else is going to get hurt because of me.\n- key: laugh but feeling like I don't really belong in the room with them.\n- key: S077\n  name: Irritability_Aggression\n  description: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.\n  manifestation: I snap at operators, supervisors, and my wife over small questions, and there have been times I've slammed a clipboard or barked at someone before I even knew I was angry.\n- key: S079\n  name: Hypervigilance\n  description: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.\n  manifestation: On the something is exploding, and it can take me several minutes before my hands stop shaking.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I've had to hand off floor responsibilities I used to manage myself, and I lost my composure during a regional director interview badly enough that I walked out. At home, my marriage feels strained because I'm present physically but not really participating in our life together.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 7 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on since the retrofit accident about 1 year and 7 months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / manager / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S076\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Has anything significant changed in your life or environment since the accident, aside from your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, some things have changed, I guess. I’m there, physically, but I’m not really participating the way I used to. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I haven’t really been feeling like myself. It’s not that anything is terribly wrong on paper, you know? My wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, things we used to enjoy, and even when something goes well at work… I just don’t really feel happy about it. Or close to her, the way I should.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but it’s been bothering me. I thought maybe I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I suppose… it’s been longer than I’d like to admit. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. I kept thinking it would sort of pass on its own, but it’s just… stayed around.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable before the accident?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still go along with things. My wife tries to get us doing our old Sunday routines, and I try to act like I’m enjoying it, because I don’t want to disappoint her. But the feeling just doesn’t really show up.\n\nEven when something good happens at work, I know I should feel pleased, and I can say the right things, but inside it’s kind of flat. I don’t feel real happiness the way I used to… or that closeness with her. I’m not sure if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I had normal ups and downs like anyone, but nothing that stuck around like this. This really seems to have started after the retrofit accident, about… a year and seven months ago. I kept hoping it would just settle down, but it hasn’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead since the accident?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of wanting to hurt myself or making any kind of plan. I don’t want to worry anyone or make it sound more dramatic than it is. I’ve just felt… not myself, I guess. But no, I haven’t been looking to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to make a big thing out of it, but I guess I am on edge a lot.\n\nAt work, when I’m on the floor, I’m constantly checking things—gauges, exits, where people’s hands are, where the emergency stops are. I tell myself it’s just being careful, being responsible, you know? But it’s… probably more than most people are doing.\n\nEven in meetings, I’ll pick a seat where I can see the door. It sounds a little excessive when I say it out loud, but it makes me feel like I can keep track of things. Is that the kind of worry you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since the accident?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes fit about the same, I think. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been a significant weight loss.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to find some pleasure or interest in things for at least a few days a week?\n\nPatient: I… I still do things, if that counts. Like my wife tries to get us back into our old Sunday routines, and I’ll go along with it. And if something goes well at work, I know I should feel good about it.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t really feel real happiness from it. Or closeness, even when she’s trying. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely shut down, but… the feeling just doesn’t seem to come the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this way without any significant improvement?\n\nPatient: I guess… since the accident. The retrofit one.\n\nIt’s been about a year and seven months now, if I’m counting right. I kept thinking it would ease up with time, and maybe I’d just get back to normal, but… not much has really changed. Does that answer what you mean by “without improvement”?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of interest in activities and feelings of detachment from your wife are present every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had other periods in your life where you felt this way before the accident?\n\nPatient: No… not like this. I mean, I’ve had rough stretches like anyone, but this specific feeling — the distance, the lack of real enjoyment — that’s really been since the retrofit accident.\n\nSo… about a year and seven months now. I don’t want to overstate it, but I can’t honestly say I remember being this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your symptoms have been consistently worse over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “consistently worse,” exactly. The last couple weeks have felt… pretty much like the same bad pattern, if that makes sense.\n\nMaybe some days stand out more than others, but I haven’t really noticed a clear shift where I’d say, “yes, it’s definitely gotten worse.” Is that the kind of answer you’re looking for?\n\nDoctor: Has your current period of feeling this way lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… not more than two years. It’s been a long time, but if I’m being accurate, it started after the retrofit accident, so about a year and seven months ago.\n\nThat’s still a while, obviously, but not past two years. Since that retrofit accident, really—about a year and seven months ago. The retrofit one.\n\nIt’s been about a year and seven months now, if I’m counting right.
